# Configuration knob removal inventory

Planning snapshot, 2026-10-26. This preserves the work so removal can resume
after worktree integration. The removals that were actually applied are listed
in [Removal log](#removal-log); everything else remains a proposal.

## Integration status

The uncommitted settings worktree was merged into `master` in `6e247ed`
(2026-10-27). That merge brought the typed resolver, root `graph_*` TOML layer,
legacy-alias warnings, startup `[SETTINGS]` binding, concise/advanced help,
Release presets, configuration guide and the settings tests into the active
checkout. CPU build, resolver tests and the Qwen migration inventory check pass
on the merged tree.

The inventory below still describes the pre-merge snapshots. Re-run the
inventory against the integrated checkout before deleting any row, and record
decisions in the JSON `review_decision` field rather than editing generated
counts by hand.

## Removal log

Scope agreed for this pass: environment variables and compile-time macros only
(no CLI or TOML surface changes), limited to controls that are provably
unreachable rather than merely unused-looking. Each entry names the evidence
that made the deletion behavior-neutral. One exception was requested
explicitly: retiring the dead cuFile feature also removes the `cuda_cufile`
TOML key (and its CLI help mention) because that key was its only consumer.

### 2026-10-27 — safe dead-knob batch

| Control | Surface | Evidence | Change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ZEROMOE_LLAMA_CPP_TAG` (K0016) | CMake input | The only reference was an `if()` that emitted "no longer used"; it never set a cache entry, path or definition. The real pin is `ZEROMOE_GGML_COMMIT`. | Deleted the warning block from `CMakeLists.txt`; `removal_status=REMOVED` in the JSON. |
| `ZEROMOE_GGML_ASYNC_GRAPH_REUSE` (K0162) | environment | Read only by `async_graph_reuse()` in `backends/ggml/ggml_hc_backend.cpp`, which had no call sites in the tree. The value it returned also OR'd a file-local `async_graph_reuse_override` that only `set_ggml_hc_async_graph_reuse()` wrote and nothing read. | Deleted the reader, the function and the write-only override. `set_ggml_hc_async_graph_reuse()` stays as a documented no-op because the shared `ggml_async_graph_reuse` TOML key is live for routed-MoE graphs (`set_ggml_moe_async_graph_reuse`). |
| `ZEROMOE_ARM_NEON` | compile definition | Defined `PUBLIC` on `kernels_cpu` for Apple Silicon, but no source, header or tool in the tree tests it. NEON kernel selection is done by CMake source selection, not by this macro. | Deleted the `target_compile_definitions(kernels_cpu PUBLIC ZEROMOE_ARM_NEON=1)` line. No consumer existed, so no build input changes meaning. |

Verification for this batch:

- `zeromoe_cpu`, `graph_settings_test` and `graph_settings_binding_test` build
  with `-DZEROMOE_CPU_ONLY=ON -DZEROMOE_USE_GGML=ON`.
- `ctest -R '^graph_settings'` passes 2/2 and
  `python3 tools/config_inventory.py --check` passes.
- `backends/ggml/ggml_hc_backend.cpp` is CUDA-only, so it is not part of the
  CPU build; it was host-syntax-checked with the CUDA build's exact defines and
  include paths (`g++ -fsyntax-only`), which is the same host compiler pass the
  real CUDA target performs for this `.cpp`.
- `python3 -c 'json.load(...)'` validates the updated
  `configuration-knob-audit.json`.

| Control | Surface | Evidence | Change |

### Findings deliberately left in place

These look dead but changing them would either alter a supported feature or
require a product decision, so they are not safe deletions for this batch:

- **`ZEROMOE_USE_LLAMA_CPP` / `ZEROMOE_LLAMA_CPP_ROOT`** are deprecated but
  live CMake aliases: they are still parsed and remapped. Deleting them is a
  compatibility break, not a dead-code removal.

## How to use

The tables contain one row per **surface/name**, not one independent behavior. The same behavior may have TOML, environment, CLI and graph aliases.
IDs are snapshot-local; track decisions by `surface` + `name` when regenerating after a merge.
Every row includes when it is read, a proposed keep/remove/combine decision, affected feature and change risk.
The companion [JSON](configuration-knob-audit.json) includes source excerpts, declaration comments/initializers, file hashes, all discovered worktree references, member-name occurrence candidates, validation requirements and `review_decision` fields (filled in for reviewed rows).
**Recommendations are source-linked candidates, not individually proven safe deletions.** A lexical occurrence cannot prove a knob obsolete or a runtime branch reachable. Exact enclosing predicates/defaults must be checked before edits.
`when used` means execution phase, not introduction date or observed recent use. No history/benchmark reachability claims are made.

## Agreed direction

- Clean break is acceptable: small public allowlist; no permanent legacy alias layer.
- Normal settings describe model, context, devices, optional resource ceilings and logging; generation/request and serving semantics remain separate.
- Derive architecture/dimensions from GGUF. Correct operators and mandatory graph paths belong to adapters. Non-GGUF formats may require additional metadata.
- Resource budgets, hotset placement, threads, staging and prefetch belong to internal planner/controller state, not hundreds of public switches.
- Keep maintained developer tooling; consolidate observational flags into INFO plus per-file DEBUG/VERBOSE. Keep explicit costly profiling/dumps and behavior-changing experiments separate.
- Build identity is generated metadata. Dependency revisions still need internal reproducible pins; logits hashes are numerical evidence, not source hashes.
- Startup calibration target: <=5 seconds on first launch; cache valid measurements. Runtime adaptation changes bounded policies, not captured tensor pointers.
- Preserve mandatory memory/graph safety and per-frame numerical correctness. The 95% performance target remains unverified.

## Existing work to preserve

The settings worktree already implements 39 canonical graph fields, strict TOML validation, provenance/legacy warnings, startup binding, Qwen placement/thread wiring, concise/advanced help, Release presets and configuration documentation.
That compatibility-first implementation is a transitional baseline, **not** the desired final public interface. The later decision favors a clean break; do not spend more work maintaining hundreds of aliases.
CPU build, settings tests and sanitizer checks passed previously. Linux/CUDA and real-model numerical/performance parity remain unverified.
The API worktree contains native OpenAI/Anthropic serving and opt-in CPU prompt checkpoints; native CUDA state caching is intentionally not claimed safe. Preserve cancellation, backpressure, authentication and state ownership.
MTP work is on its own branch. Do not remove draft/state-related controls as if that branch were already integrated.
Automatic startup calibration, runtime adaptation and file-scoped logging are planned, not implemented by this audit.

## Manually checked exceptions

| Control | What source confirms | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| `ZEROMOE_THREAD_PROFILE`, `_RT`, `_APPLY_NICE` | `core/thread_profile.hpp:3-6,39-52`: changes placement/priority, not merely output. | Explicit developer execution policy; never enable via verbosity. |
| `ZEROMOE_DECODE_LOGITS_HASH` | `core/generation_loop.hpp:107-112`: live decode logits fingerprint. | Keep/combine numerical validation capability, not a source-version flag. |
| `ZEROMOE_GGML_COMMIT` | `CMakeLists.txt`: selects revision fetched when local ggml is unavailable. | Internal reproducible dependency pin; do not delete dependency identity. |
| `ZEROMOE_LLAMA_CPP_TAG` | CMake references only emit a warning that it is no longer used. | Remove the warning-only compatibility input; keep the real dependency pin. |
| `ZEROMOE_API_KEY` | API `backends/cpu/cpu_main.cpp:93-96`: secret read and non-loopback authentication requirement. | Preserve authentication input; never log its value. |
| `model family` | `models/base/model_family.hpp`: detection already uses GGUF architecture and tensor metadata. | No new family-selection knob; preserve loader/adapter validation. |
| `trace_filter`, `trace_abort_on_nan` | `core/tensor_trace.hpp`: filtering is gated by VERBOSE; abort mode changes failure behavior. | Scoped logging can replace output filtering, but abort mode remains explicit. |

## Risk and action semantics

| Risk | Meaning |
|---|---|
| LOW | Primarily help/output/internal bookkeeping; still check parsing and consumers. |
| MEDIUM | Can break request semantics, scripts, observability or workload performance. |
| HIGH | May affect model numerics, state, graph bindings, asynchronous memory lifetime, OOM safety, platform builds or major performance policy. |

**INTERNALIZE** means remove the external selector, retain its required behavior in typed engine state, and assign an owner. It does not mean delete the algorithm.
**COMBINE** means preserve independent semantics while unifying input/diagnostics; verbosity must not activate execution experiments.
**KEEP DEV/TOOL** is conditional on a maintained use; it is not a requirement to keep old names.
No row is automatically safe to delete based on risk alone. No engine runtime flag is declared proven dead; the warning-only build compatibility input is a separately checked exception.

## Removal batches

1. Integrate/rebase worktrees and define the public allowlist; keep dirty baselines intact.
2. Consolidate scoped observational logging/help. Keep dumps, synchronization checks and numerical validation separate.
3. Internalize adapter/backend correctness and derive GGUF facts. Preserve measured recipes as internal policy tests, not hundreds of required settings.
4. Replace memory/thread/prefetch/placement inputs with startup planner outputs; retain explicit resource ceilings and mandatory reserves.
5. Introduce conservative runtime policy adaptation within preallocated resources, with cooldowns/backoff and safe concurrency.
6. Remove superseded parsers, aliases and unused experiments in coherent batches; migrate maintained scripts. Unknown removed input must fail explicitly.

Validate each behavioral batch with per-decode-frame teacher forcing, applicable kernel checks, graph full-fit conformance, memory-pressure cases and phase-separated performance. Do not combine all deletions and tuning changes into one unreviewable patch.

## Dynamic lookup follow-up

Indirect `getenv(name)`/registry lookups and family namespace prefixes are not independent knobs. The JSON lists all discovered locations under `dynamic_lookups`; expanding each constructed name requires tracing its caller/registry.
The typed graph aliases are linked to their registered canonical fields where present. Other dynamically assembled names may not be fully enumerated.

## Resume checklist

- Start from the integrated revision; compare the JSON snapshot/file hashes before trusting line numbers.
- Choose an ownership batch, not a random list of low-risk names.
- Fill `review_decision`, identify maintained consumers and replacement state, then remove parser/wiring/scripts together.
- Do not copy experimental values or defaults across model families without checking equations/capabilities.
- Record actual external controls removed, internalized and combined separately; do not count renamed aliases as deletions.
- Linux/CUDA plus real weights are required before claiming graph correctness/performance parity.
